## Names

### Introduction

The Name of an entity is used in place of the handle in authorization
computations. The substitution occurs in *cpHash* and *policyHash*
computations.

For an entity that is defined by a public area (objects and NV Indexes),
the Name is the hash of the public structure that defines the entity.
The hash is done using the *nameAlg* of the entity.

::: Note :::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::
For an object, a `TPMT_PUBLIC` defines the entity. For an NV Index,
a `TPMS_NV_PUBLIC` defines the entity.
::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::

For entities not defined by a public area, the Name is the handle that
is used to refer to the entity.

### TPMU_NAME {#sec:TPMU-NAME}

@tbl:placeholder-table-98 defines the `TPMU_NAME` union.

Table: Definition of TPMU_NAME Union {#tbl:placeholder-table-98}

+----------------------+-----------------------+------------+----------------------------------------------------------+
| Parameter            | Type                  | Selector   | Description                                              |
+======================+=======================+============+==========================================================+
| digest               | [TPMT_HA](            |            | when the Name is a digest                                |
|                      | #sec:TPMT-HA)         |            |                                                          |
+----------------------+-----------------------+------------+----------------------------------------------------------+
| handle               | [TPM_HANDLE](         |            | when the Name is a handle                                |
|                      | #sec:TPM-HANDLE)      |            |                                                          |
+----------------------+-----------------------+------------+----------------------------------------------------------+


### TPM2B_NAME {#sec:TPM2B-NAME}

The @tbl:placeholder-table-99 buffer holds a Name for any entity type.

The type of Name in the structure is determined by context and the
*size* parameter. If *size* is four, then the Name is a handle. If
*size* is zero, then no Name is present. Otherwise, the size shall be
the size of a `TPM_ALG_ID` plus the size of the digest produced by the
indicated hash algorithm.

Table: Definition of TPM2B_NAME Structure {#tbl:placeholder-table-99}

+--------------------------------------------+-----------------------+-------------------------------------------------+
| Parameter                                  | Type                  | Description                                     |
+============================================+=======================+=================================================+
| size                                       | UINT16                | size of the Name structure                      |
+--------------------------------------------+-----------------------+-------------------------------------------------+
| name\[size\]{:sizeof([TPMU_NAME](          | BYTE                  | the Name structure                              |
| #sec:TPMU-NAME))}                          |                       |                                                 |
+--------------------------------------------+-----------------------+-------------------------------------------------+


## PCR Structures

### TPMS_PCR_SELECT {#sec:TPMS-PCR-SELECT}

The @tbl:placeholder-table-100 structure provides a standard method of specifying a list
of PCR.

PCR numbering starts at zero.

*pcrSelect* is an array of octets. The octet containing the bit
corresponding to a specific PCR is found by dividing the PCR number by
8.

::: Example ::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::
The bit in *pcrSelect* corresponding to PCR 19 is in
*pcrSelect* \[2\] (19/8 = 2).
::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::

The least significant bit in an octet is bit number 0. The bit in the
octet associated with a PCR is the remainder after division by 8.

::: Example ::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::
The bit in *pcrSelect* \[2\] corresponding to PCR 19 is bit 3
(19 mod 8). If *sizeofSelect* is 3, then the *pcrSelect* array that
would specify PCR 19 and no other PCR is ${\text{00 00 08}}_{16}$.
::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::

Each bit in *pcrSelect* indicates whether the corresponding PCR is
selected (1) or not (0). If the *pcrSelect* is all zero bits, then no
PCR is selected.

*sizeofSelect* indicates the number of octets in *pcrSelect*. The
allowable value for *sizeofSelect* is determined by the number of PCR
required by the applicable platform-specific specification and the
number of PCR implemented in the TPM. The minimum value for
*sizeofSelect* is:

$$ \mathrm{PCR\_SELECT\_MIN} ≔ (\mathrm{PLATFORM\_PCR} + 7) / 8 $$ {#eq:pcr-select-min}

where

: {.plain .unnumbered .unlisted}

+---+--------------------------+----------------------------------------------------------------------+
|   | $\mathrm{PLATFORM\_PCR}$ | is the number of PCR required by the platform-specific specification |
+---+--------------------------+----------------------------------------------------------------------+

The maximum value for *sizeofSelect* is:

$$ \mathrm{PCR\_SELECT\_MAX} ≔ (\mathrm{IMPLEMENTATION\_PCR} + 7) / 8 $$ {#eq:pcr-select-max}

where

: {.plain .unnumbered .unlisted}

+---+--------------------------------+---------------------------------------------+
|   | $\mathrm{IMPLEMENTATION\_PCR}$ | is the number of PCR implemented on the TPM |
+---+--------------------------------+---------------------------------------------+

If the TPM implements more PCR than there are bits in *pcrSelect*, the
additional PCR are not selected.

::: Example ::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::
If the applicable platform-specific specification requires
that the TPM have a minimum of 24 PCR but the TPM implements 32, then a
PCR select of 3 octets would imply that PCR 24-31 are not selected.
::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::

Table: Definition of TPMS_PCR_SELECT Structure {#tbl:placeholder-table-100}

+--------------------------------------------------+-------------------------+-----------------------------------------+
| Parameter                                        | Type                    | Description                             |
+==================================================+=========================+=========================================+
| sizeofSelect {PCR_SELECT_MIN:}                   | UINT8                   | the size in octets of the *pcrSelect*   |
|                                                  |                         | array                                   |
+--------------------------------------------------+-------------------------+-----------------------------------------+
| pcrSelect \[sizeofSelect\] {:PCR_SELECT_MAX}     | BYTE                    | the bit map of selected PCR             |
+--------------------------------------------------+-------------------------+-----------------------------------------+
| #TPM_RC_VALUE                                    |                         |                                         |
+--------------------------------------------------+-------------------------+-----------------------------------------+


### TPMS_PCR_SELECTION {#sec:TPMS-PCR-SELECTION}

@tbl:placeholder-table-101 defines the TPMS_PCR_SELECTION Structure.

Table: Definition of TPMS_PCR_SELECTION Structure {#tbl:placeholder-table-101}

+--------------------------------------------------+-------------------------+-----------------------------------------+
| Parameter                                        | Type                    | Description                             |
+==================================================+=========================+=========================================+
| hash                                             | [TPMI_ALG_HASH](        | the hash algorithm associated with the  |
|                                                  | #sec:TPMI-ALG-HASH)     | selection                               |
+--------------------------------------------------+-------------------------+-----------------------------------------+
| sizeofSelect {PCR_SELECT_MIN:}                   | UINT8                   | the size in octets of the *pcrSelect*   |
|                                                  |                         | array                                   |
+--------------------------------------------------+-------------------------+-----------------------------------------+
| pcrSelect \[sizeofSelect\] {:PCR_SELECT_MAX}     | BYTE                    | the bit map of selected PCR             |
+--------------------------------------------------+-------------------------+-----------------------------------------+
| #TPM_RC_VALUE                                    |                         |                                         |
+--------------------------------------------------+-------------------------+-----------------------------------------+


## Tickets {#sec:tickets}

### Introduction

Tickets are evidence that the TPM has previously processed some
information. A ticket is an HMAC over the data using a secret key known
only to the TPM. A ticket is a way to expand the state memory of the
TPM. A ticket is only usable by the TPM that produced it.

The formulations for tickets shown in @sec:tickets are to be used by a TPM that
is compliant with this specification.

The method of creating the ticket data is:

$$ \mathbf{HMAC}_\mathit{contextAlg}(\mathit{proof}, (\mathit{ticketType} ∥ \mathit{param} \thickspace\{ ∥ \mathit{param} \thickspace\{ \ldots \}\})) $$ {#eq:method-to-create-ticket} 

where

: {.plain .unnumbered .unlisted}

+---+-------------------------------------+------------------------------------------------------+
|   | $\mathbf{HMAC}_\mathit{contextAlg}$ | is an HMAC using the hash used for context integrity |
+---+-------------------------------------+------------------------------------------------------+
|   | $\mathit{proof}$                    | is a TPM secret value (depends on hierarchy)         |
+---+-------------------------------------+------------------------------------------------------+
|   | $\mathit{ticketType}$               | is a value to differentiate the tickets              |
+---+-------------------------------------+------------------------------------------------------+
|   | $\mathit{param}$                    | are one or more values that were checked by the TPM  |
+---+-------------------------------------+------------------------------------------------------+

The proof value used for each hierarchy is shown in @tbl:placeholder-table-102.

Table: Values for proof Used in Tickets {#tbl:placeholder-table-102}

+-------------------+----------------------------+---------------------------------------------------------------------+
| Hierarchy         | proof                      | Description                                                         |
+===================+============================+=====================================================================+
| Null              | nullProof                  | a value that changes with every TPM Reset                           |
+-------------------+----------------------------+---------------------------------------------------------------------+
| Platform          | phProof                    | a value that changes with each change of the PPS                    |
+-------------------+----------------------------+---------------------------------------------------------------------+
| Owner             | shProof                    | a value that changes with each change of the SPS                    |
+-------------------+----------------------------+---------------------------------------------------------------------+
| Endorsement       | ehProof                    | a value that changes with each change of either the EPS or SPS      |
+-------------------+----------------------------+---------------------------------------------------------------------+


The format for a ticket is shown in @tbl:placeholder-table-103. This is a template for
the tickets shown in the remainder of @sec:tickets.

Table: General Format of a Ticket {#tbl:placeholder-table-103}

+---------------------------+------------------------------+-----------------------------------------------------------+
| Parameter                 | Type                         | Description                                               |
+===========================+==============================+===========================================================+
| tag                       | [TPM_ST](#sec:TPM-ST)        | structure tag indicating the type of the ticket           |
+---------------------------+------------------------------+-----------------------------------------------------------+
| hierarchy                 | [TPMI_RH_HIERARCHY](         | the hierarchy of the proof value                          |
|                           | #sec:TPMI-RH-HIERARCHY)+     |                                                           |
+---------------------------+------------------------------+-----------------------------------------------------------+
| digest                    | [TPM2B_DIGEST](              | the HMAC over the ticket-specific data                    |
|                           | #sec:TPM2B-DIGEST)           |                                                           |
+---------------------------+------------------------------+-----------------------------------------------------------+


### A NULL Ticket

When a command requires a ticket and no ticket is available, the caller
is required to provide a structure with a ticket *tag* that is correct
for the context. The *hierarchy* shall be set to `TPM_RH_NULL`, and
*digest* shall be the Empty Buffer (a buffer with a size field of zero).
This construct is the NULL Ticket. When a response indicates that a
ticket is returned, the TPM may return a NULL Ticket.

::: Note :::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::
Because each use of a ticket requires that the structure tag for
the ticket be appropriate for the use, there is no single representation
of a NULL Ticket that will work in all circumstances. Minimally, a NULL
ticket will have a structure type that is appropriate for the context.
::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::

### TPMT_TK_CREATION {#sec:TPMT-TK-CREATION}

The @tbl:placeholder-table-104 ticket is produced by `TPM2_Create()` or
`TPM2_CreatePrimary()`. It is used to bind the creation data to the
object to which it applies. The ticket is computed by

<!-- two spaces at end of math line create a line break -->
$$ \mathbf{HMAC}_{\mathit{contextAlg}}(\mathit{proof}, (\mathrm{TPM\_ST\_CREATION} ∥ \mathit{name} ∥  
   \mathbf{H}_{\mathit{nameAlg}}(\mathrm{TPMS\_CREATION\_DATA}))) $$ {#eq:ticket-tk-creation}

where

: {.plain .unnumbered .unlisted}

+---+----------------------------------+------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
|   | **HMAC**$_{\mathit{contextAlg}}$ | an HMAC using the context integrity hash algorithm                           |
+---+----------------------------------+------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
|   | proof                            | a TPM secret value associated with the hierarchy                             |
|   |                                  | associated with name                                                         |
+---+----------------------------------+------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
|   | `TPM_ST_CREATION`                | a value used to ensure that the ticket is properly                           |
|   |                                  | used                                                                         |
+---+----------------------------------+------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
|   | name                             | the Name of the object to which the creation data                            |
|   |                                  | is to be associated                                                          |
+---+----------------------------------+------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
|   | **H**$_{\mathit{nameAlg}}$       | hash using the nameAlg of the created object                                 |
+---+----------------------------------+------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
|   | `TPMS_CREATION_DATA`             | the creation data structure associated with name                             |
+---+----------------------------------+------------------------------------------------------------------------------+

Table: Definition of TPMT_TK_CREATION Structure {#tbl:placeholder-table-104}

+---------------------------------+----------------------------+-------------------------------------------------------+
| Parameter                       | Type                       | Description                                           |
+=================================+============================+=======================================================+
| tag {TPM_ST_CREATION}           | [TPM_ST](#sec:TPM-ST)      | ticket structure tag                                  |
+---------------------------------+----------------------------+-------------------------------------------------------+
| #TPM_RC_TAG                     |                            | error returned when *tag* is not TPM_ST_CREATION      |
+---------------------------------+----------------------------+-------------------------------------------------------+
| hierarchy                       | [TPMI_RH_HIERARCHY](       | the hierarchy containing *name*                       |
|                                 | #sec:TPMI-RH-HIERARCHY)+   |                                                       |
+---------------------------------+----------------------------+-------------------------------------------------------+
| digest                          | [TPM2B_DIGEST](            | This shall be the HMAC produced using a proof value   |
|                                 | #sec:TPM2B-DIGEST)         | of                                                    |
|                                 |                            | *hierarchy*.                                          |
+---------------------------------+----------------------------+-------------------------------------------------------+


::: Example ::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::
A NULL Creation Ticket is the tuple <`TPM_ST_CREATION`,
`TPM_RH_NULL`, 0x0000>.
::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::

### TPMU_TK_VERIFIED_META {#sec:TPMU-TK-VERIFIED-META}

The @tbl:tpmu-tk-verified-metadata `TPMU_TK_VERIFIED_META` contains additional
metadata for a `TPMT_TK_VERIFIED`. Some types of `TPMT_TK_VERIFIED` (identified
by their associated `TPM_ST` value) do not have any additional metadata.

Table: Definition of TPMU_TK_VERIFIED_META Union {#tbl:tpmu-tk-verified-metadata}

+-------------------+---------------------+--------------------------------------+---------------------------------+
| Parameter         | Type                | Selector                             | Description                     |
+===================+=====================+======================================+=================================+
| verified          | [TPMS_EMPTY](       | `TPM_ST_VERIFIED`                    |                                 |
|                   | #sec:TPMS-EMPTY)    |                                      |                                 |
+-------------------+---------------------+--------------------------------------+---------------------------------+
| messageVerified   | [TPMS_EMPTY](       | `TPM_ST_MESSAGE_VERIFIED`            |                                 |
|                   | #sec:TPMS-EMPTY)    |                                      |                                 |
+-------------------+---------------------+--------------------------------------+---------------------------------+
| digestVerified    | [TPMI_ALG_HASH](    | `TPM_ST_DIGEST_VERIFIED`             | The hash algorithm or XOF used  |
|                   | #sec:TPMI-ALG-HASH) |                                      | to produce the digest that was  |
|                   |                     |                                      | verified                        |
+-------------------+---------------------+--------------------------------------+---------------------------------+

<!--
    When adding more members to this union, consider that the union is included
    in the HMAC (eq:ticket-tk-verified).
    All the current members of this union are fixed size for a given selector
    value. To minimize the risk of type punning in the future, let's ensure that
    future non-fixed-size members of TPMU_TK_VERIFIED_META (e.g., a TPM2B) are
    fully included in the HMAC, including the size field.
-->

### TPMT_TK_VERIFIED {#sec:TPMT-TK-VERIFIED}

The @tbl:tpmt-tk-verified ticket is produced by `TPM2_VerifySignature()`,
`TPM2_VerifySequenceComplete()`, and `TPM2_VerifyDigestSignature()`. It is used
in `TPM2_PolicyAuthorize()`.

The ticket provides evidence that the TPM has validated that a message or digest
was signed by a key with the Name of *keyName*. The ticket contains an HMAC,
which is computed by

$$ \mathbf{HMAC}_{\mathit{contextAlg}}(\mathit{proof}, (\mathit{tag} \parallel 
    \mathit{digestOrMessage} \parallel \mathit{keyName} \parallel \mathit{metadata})) $$ {#eq:ticket-tk-verified}

where

: {.plain .unnumbered .unlisted}

+---+----------------------------------+----------------------------------------------------------+
|   | **HMAC**$_{\mathit{contextAlg}}$ | an HMAC using the context integrity hash                 |
+---+----------------------------------+----------------------------------------------------------+
|   | proof                            | a TPM secret value associated with the                   |
|   |                                  | hierarchy associated with *keyName*                      |
+---+----------------------------------+----------------------------------------------------------+
|   | tag                              | see @tbl:tpm-st-for-signatures                           |
+---+----------------------------------+----------------------------------------------------------+
|   | digestOrMessage                  | the signed digest or message                             |
+---+----------------------------------+----------------------------------------------------------+
|   | keyName                          | Name of the key that signed *digestOrMessage*            |
+---+----------------------------------+----------------------------------------------------------+
|   | metadata                         | Additional metadata depending on the type of ticket      |
|   |                                  | (may be empty-length)                                    |
+---+----------------------------------+----------------------------------------------------------+

The *tag* value depends on which TPM command produced the ticket.
@tbl:tpm-st-for-signatures lists the values and associated commands.

The *metadata* is the serialized
[TPMU_TK_VERIFIED_META](#sec:TPMU-TK-VERIFIED-META) contents according to the
*tag* value. For some cases, *metadata* is zero-length.

Table: TPM_ST Values For Signature Verification Commands {#tbl:tpm-st-for-signatures}

+---------------------------------+--------------------------------------------+
| *tag* value                     | Command                                    |
+=================================+============================================+
| `TPM_ST_VERIFIED`               | `TPM2_VerifySignature()`                   |
+---------------------------------+--------------------------------------------+
| `TPM_ST_MESSAGE_VERIFIED`       | `TPM2_VerifySequenceComplete()`            |
+---------------------------------+--------------------------------------------+
| `TPM_ST_DIGEST_VERIFIED`        | `TPM2_VerifyDigestSignature()`             |
+---------------------------------+--------------------------------------------+

Table: Definition of TPMT_TK_VERIFIED Structure {#tbl:tpmt-tk-verified}

+---------------------------------+---------------------------------+--------------------------------------------------+
| Parameter                       | Type                            | Description                                      |
+=================================+=================================+==================================================+
| tag                             | [TPM_ST](#sec:TPM-ST)           | ticket structure tag                             |
+---------------------------------+---------------------------------+--------------------------------------------------+
| #TPM_RC_TAG                     |                                 | error returned when *tag* is not one of          |
|                                 |                                 | the values in @tbl:tpm-st-for-signatures         |
+---------------------------------+---------------------------------+--------------------------------------------------+
| hierarchy                       | [TPMI_RH_HIERARCHY](            | the hierarchy containing *keyName*.              |
|                                 | #sec:TPMI-RH-HIERARCHY)+        |                                                  |
+---------------------------------+---------------------------------+--------------------------------------------------+
| \[tag\]metadata                 | [TPMU_TK_VERIFIED_META](        | Additional (if any) metadata required in order   |
|                                 | #sec:TPMU-TK-VERIFIED-META)     | to validate the ticket.                          |
+---------------------------------+---------------------------------+--------------------------------------------------+
| hmac                            | [TPM2B_DIGEST](                 | This shall be the HMAC produced using a proof    |
|                                 | #sec:TPM2B-DIGEST)              | value of *hierarchy*.                            |
|                                 |                                 |                                                  |
|                                 |                                 | ::: NOTE ::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::: |
|                                 |                                 | Earlier versions of the TPM 2.0 specification    |
|                                 |                                 | called this field *digest*. It was renamed in    |
|                                 |                                 | version 185 to reduce ambiguity, since there are |
|                                 |                                 | other digests associated with a                  |
|                                 |                                 | `TPMT_TK_VERIFIED`.                              |
|                                 |                                 | :::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::: |
+---------------------------------+---------------------------------+--------------------------------------------------+

::: Example ::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::
A NULL Verified Ticket is the tuple <*tag*, `TPM_RH_NULL`, 0x0000>, where *tag*
is one of `TPM_ST_VERIFIED`, `TPM_ST_MESSAGE_VERIFIED`, or
`TPM_ST_DIGEST_VERIFIED`.
::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::

### TPMT_TK_AUTH {#sec:TPMT-TK-AUTH}

This @tbl:placeholder-table-106 ticket is produced by `TPM2_PolicySigned()` and
`TPM2_PolicySecret()` when the authorization has an expiration time. If
*nonceTPM* was provided in the policy command, the ticket is computed
by

$$ \mathbf{HMAC}_{\mathit{contextAlg}}(\mathit{proof}, (\mathrm{TPM\_ST\_AUTH\_xxx} ∥
    cpHash ∥ policyRef ∥ authName  
 ∥ timeout ∥ [timeEpoch] ∥ [resetCount])) $$ {#eq:ticket-tk-auth}

where

: {.plain .unnumbered .unlisted}

+---+----------------------------------+----------------------------------------------------------+
|   | **HMAC**$_{\mathit{contextAlg}}$ | an HMAC using the context integrity hash                 |
+---+----------------------------------+----------------------------------------------------------+
|   | proof                            | a TPM secret value associated with the                   |
|   |                                  | hierarchy of the object associated with                  |
|   |                                  | authName                                                 |
+---+----------------------------------+----------------------------------------------------------+
|   | `TPM_ST_AUTH_xxx`                | either `TPM_ST_AUTH_SIGNED` or                           |
|   |                                  | `TPM_ST_AUTH_SECRET`; used to ensure that                |
|   |                                  | the ticket is properly used                              |
+---+----------------------------------+----------------------------------------------------------+
|   | cpHash                           | optional hash of the authorized command                  |
+---+----------------------------------+----------------------------------------------------------+
|   | policyRef                        | optional reference to a policy value                     |
+---+----------------------------------+----------------------------------------------------------+
|   | authName                         | Name of the object that signed the                       |
|   |                                  | authorization                                            |
+---+----------------------------------+----------------------------------------------------------+
|   | timeout                          | implementation-specific value indicating                 |
|   |                                  | when the authorization expires                           |
+---+----------------------------------+----------------------------------------------------------+
|   | timeEpoch                        | implementation-specific representation of                |
|   |                                  | the timeEpoch at the time the ticket was                 |
|   |                                  | created                                                  |
|   |                                  |                                                          |
|   |                                  | ::: Note ::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::: |
|   |                                  | This is not included if *timeout* is zero.               |
|   |                                  | :::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::: |
+---+----------------------------------+----------------------------------------------------------+
|   | resetCount                       | implementation-specific representation of                |
|   |                                  | the TPM's totalResetCount                                |
|   |                                  |                                                          |
|   |                                  | ::: Note ::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::: |
|   |                                  | This is not included if *timeout* is zero                |
|   |                                  | or if *nonceTPM* was include in the                      |
|   |                                  | authorization.                                           |
|   |                                  | :::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::: |
+---+----------------------------------+----------------------------------------------------------+

Table: Definition of TPMT_TK_AUTH Structure {#tbl:placeholder-table-106}

+------------------------------------------+----------------------------------------+----------------------------------+
| Parameter                                | Type                                   | Description                      |
+==========================================+========================================+==================================+
| tag {TPM_ST_AUTH_SIGNED,                 | [TPM_ST](#sec:TPM-ST)                  | ticket structure tag             |
| TPM_ST_AUTH_SECRET}                      |                                        |                                  |
+------------------------------------------+----------------------------------------+----------------------------------+
| #TPM_RC_TAG                              |                                        | error returned when *tag* is not |
|                                          |                                        | one of the allowed values        |
+------------------------------------------+----------------------------------------+----------------------------------+
| hierarchy                                | [TPMI_RH_HIERARCHY](                   | the hierarchy of the object used |
|                                          | #sec:TPMI-RH-HIERARCHY)+               | to produce the ticket            |
+------------------------------------------+----------------------------------------+----------------------------------+
| digest                                   | [TPM2B_DIGEST](#sec:TPM2B-DIGEST)      | This shall be the HMAC produced  |
|                                          |                                        | using a proof value of           |
|                                          |                                        | *hierarchy*.                     |
+------------------------------------------+----------------------------------------+----------------------------------+


::: Example ::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::
A NULL Auth Ticket is the tuple <`TPM_ST_AUTH_SIGNED`,
`TPM_RH_NULL`, 0x0000> or the tuple <`TPM_ST_AUTH_SIGNED`,
`TPM_RH_NULL`, 0x0000>
::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::

### TPMT_TK_HASHCHECK {#sec:TPMT-TK-HASHCHECK}

This @tbl:placeholder-table-107 ticket is produced by `TPM2_SequenceComplete()` or
`TPM2_Hash()` when the message that was digested did not start with
`TPM_GENERATED_VALUE`. The ticket is computed by

$$ \mathbf{HMAC}_{\mathit{contextAlg}}(\mathit{proof}, (\mathrm{TPM\_ST\_HASHCHECK} ∥ \mathit{digest})) $$ {#eq:ticket-tk-hashcheck}

where

: {.plain .unnumbered .unlisted}

+---+---------------------------------------+----------------------------------------------+
|   | $\mathbf{HMAC}_{\mathit{contextAlg}}$ | is an HMAC using the context integrity hash  |
+---+---------------------------------------+----------------------------------------------+
|   | $\mathit{proof}$                      | is a TPM secret value associated with the    |
|   |                                       | hierarchy indicated by the command           |
+---+---------------------------------------+----------------------------------------------+
|   | $\mathrm{TPM\_ST\_HASHCHECK}$         | is a value used to ensure that the ticket is |
|   |                                       | properly used                                |
+---+---------------------------------------+----------------------------------------------+
|   | $\mathit{digest}$                     | is the digest of the data                    |
+---+---------------------------------------+----------------------------------------------+

Table: Definition of TPMT_TK_HASHCHECK Structure {#tbl:placeholder-table-107}

+---------------------------------+-------------------------------+----------------------------------------------------+
| Parameter                       | Type                          | Description                                        |
+=================================+===============================+====================================================+
| tag {TPM_ST_HASHCHECK}          | [TPM_ST](#sec:TPM-ST)         | ticket structure tag                               |
+---------------------------------+-------------------------------+----------------------------------------------------+
| #TPM_RC_TAG                     |                               | error returned when is not TPM_ST_HASHCHECK        |
+---------------------------------+-------------------------------+----------------------------------------------------+
| hierarchy                       | [TPMI_RH_HIERARCHY](          | the hierarchy                                      |
|                                 | #sec:TPMI-RH-HIERARCHY)+      |                                                    |
+---------------------------------+-------------------------------+----------------------------------------------------+
| digest                          | [TPM2B_DIGEST](               | This shall be the HMAC produced using a proof      |
|                                 | #sec:TPM2B-DIGEST)            | value of                                           |
|                                 |                               | *hierarchy*.                                       |
+---------------------------------+-------------------------------+----------------------------------------------------+

### TPMS_SPDM_SESSION_INFO {#sec:TPMS-SPDM-SESSION-INFO}

## Lists

### TPML_CC {#sec:TPML-CC}

The @tbl:placeholder-table-113 list of command codes may be input to the TPM or returned
by the TPM depending on the command.

Table: Definition of TPML_CC Structure {#tbl:placeholder-table-113}

+--------------------------------------------------+---------------+---------------------------------------------------+
| Parameter                                        | Type          | Description                                       |
+==================================================+===============+===================================================+
| count                                            | UINT32        | number of commands in the *commandCode* list; may |
|                                                  |               | be 0                                              |
+--------------------------------------------------+---------------+---------------------------------------------------+
| commandCodes[count]{:MAX_CAP_CC}                 | [TPM_CC](     | a list of command codes                           |
|                                                  | #sec:TPM-CC)  |                                                   |
|                                                  |               | The maximum only applies to a command code list   |
|                                                  |               | in a command. The response size is limited only   |
|                                                  |               | by the size of the parameter buffer.              |
+--------------------------------------------------+---------------+---------------------------------------------------+
| #TPM_RC_SIZE                                     |               | response code when count is greater than the      |
|                                                  |               | maximum allowed list size                         |
+--------------------------------------------------+---------------+---------------------------------------------------+


### TPML_CCA {#sec:TPML-CCA}

This @tbl:placeholder-table-114 list is only used in `TPM2_GetCapability`(*capability* ==
`TPM_CAP_COMMANDS`).

The values in the list are returned in `TPMA_CC`->*commandIndex* order
(see @tbl:placeholder-table-39) with vendor-specific commands returned after other
commands. Because of the other attributes, the commands may not be
returned in strict numerical order.

Table: Definition of TPML_CCA Structure  {#tbl:placeholder-table-114}

+--------------------------------------------------+---------------+---------------------------------------------------+
| Parameter                                        | Type          | Description                                       |
+==================================================+===============+===================================================+
| count                                            | UINT32        | number of values in the *commandAttributes* list; |
|                                                  |               | may be 0                                          |
+--------------------------------------------------+---------------+---------------------------------------------------+
| commandAttributes[count]{:MAX_CAP_CC}            | [TPMA_CC](    | a list of command codes attributes                |
|                                                  | #sec:TPMA-CC) |                                                   |
+--------------------------------------------------+---------------+---------------------------------------------------+


### TPML_ALG {#sec:TPML-ALG}

This @tbl:placeholder-table-115 list is returned by `TPM2_IncrementalSelfTest()`.

+------------------------------------------------+-----------------+---------------------------------------------------+

|                                                |                 | a command. The response size is limited only by   |
|                                                |                 | the size of the parameter buffer.                 |
+------------------------------------------------+-----------------+---------------------------------------------------+
| #TPM_RC_SIZE                                   |                 | response code when *count* is greater than the    |
|                                                |                 | maximum allowed list size                         |
+------------------------------------------------+-----------------+---------------------------------------------------+


### TPML_HANDLE {#sec:TPML-HANDLE}

This @tbl:placeholder-table-116 structure is used when the TPM returns a list of loaded

::: Note :::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::
`MAX_CAP_HANDLES` = (`MAX_CAP_DATA` / sizeof(`TPM_HANDLE`))
::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::

|                                            |                  | may have a value of 0                                |
+--------------------------------------------+------------------+------------------------------------------------------+

|                                            | #sec:TPM-HANDLE) |                                                      |
+--------------------------------------------+------------------+------------------------------------------------------+
| #TPM_RC_SIZE                               |                  | response code when *count* is greater than the       |
|                                            |                  | maximum allowed list size                            |
+--------------------------------------------+------------------+------------------------------------------------------+


### TPML_DIGEST {#sec:TPML-DIGEST}

This @tbl:placeholder-table-117 list is used to convey a list of digest values. This type
is used in `TPM2_PolicyOR()` and in `TPM2_PCR_Read()`.

Table: Definition of TPML_DIGEST Structure {#tbl:placeholder-table-117}

+------------------------------------------+---------------------+-----------------------------------------------------+
| Parameter                                | Type                | Description                                         |
+==========================================+=====================+=====================================================+
| count {2:}                               | UINT32              | number of digests in the list                       |
|                                          |                     |                                                     |
|                                          |                     | The minimum is two for TPM2_PolicyOR().             |
+------------------------------------------+---------------------+-----------------------------------------------------+
| digests[count]{:8}                       | [TPM2B_DIGEST](     | a list of digests                                   |
|                                          | #sec:TPM2B-DIGEST)  |                                                     |
|                                          |                     | For TPM2_PolicyOR(), all digests will have been     |
|                                          |                     | computed using the digest of the policy session.    |
|                                          |                     | For TPM2_PCR_Read(), each digest will be the size   |
|                                          |                     | of the digest for the bank containing the PCR.      |
+------------------------------------------+---------------------+-----------------------------------------------------+
| #TPM_RC_SIZE                             |                     | response code when *count* is not at least two or   |
|                                          |                     | is greater than eight                               |
+------------------------------------------+---------------------+-----------------------------------------------------+


### TPML_DIGEST_VALUES {#sec:TPML-DIGEST-VALUES}

This @tbl:placeholder-table-118 list is used to convey a list of digest values. This type
is returned by `TPM2_PCR_Event()` and `TPM2_EventSequenceComplete()` and
is an input for `TPM2_PCR_Extend()`.

::: Note :::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::
This construct limits the number of hashes in the list to the
number of digests implemented in the TPM rather than the number of PCR
banks. This allows extra values to appear in a call to
`TPM2_PCR_Extend()`.
::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::

::: Note :::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::
The digest for an unimplemented hash algorithm may not be in a
list because the TPM may not recognize the algorithm as being a hash and
it may not know the digest size.
::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::

Table: Definition of TPML_DIGEST_VALUES Structure {#tbl:placeholder-table-118}

+------------------------------------------+------------------+--------------------------------------------------------+
| Parameter                                | Type             | Description                                            |
+==========================================+==================+========================================================+
| count                                    | UINT32           | number of digests in the list                          |
+------------------------------------------+------------------+--------------------------------------------------------+
| digests[count]{:HASH_COUNT}              | [TPMT_HA](       | a list of tagged digests                               |
|                                          | #sec:TPMT-HA)    |                                                        |
+------------------------------------------+------------------+--------------------------------------------------------+
| #TPM_RC_SIZE                             |                  | response code when *count* is greater than the         |
|                                          |                  | possible number of banks                               |
+------------------------------------------+------------------+--------------------------------------------------------+


### TPML_PCR_SELECTION {#sec:TPML-PCR-SELECTION}

This @tbl:placeholder-table-119 list is used to indicate the PCR that are included in a
selection when more than one PCR value may be selected.

This structure is an input parameter to `TPM2_PolicyPCR()` to indicate
the PCR that will be included in the digest of PCR for the
authorization. The structure is used in `TPM2_PCR_Read()` command to
indicate the PCR values to be returned and in the response to indicate
which PCR are included in the list of returned digests. The structure is
an output parameter from `TPM2_Create()` and indicates the PCR used in
the digest of the PCR state when the object was created. The structure
is also contained in the attestation structure of `TPM2_Quote()`.

When this structure is used to select PCR to be included in a digest,
the selected PCR are concatenated to create a "message" containing all
of the PCR, and then the message is hashed using the context-specific
hash algorithm.

Table: Definition of TPML_PCR_SELECTION Structure {#tbl:placeholder-table-119}

+---------------------------------------------+-----------------------------+------------------------------------------+
| Parameter                                   | Type                        | Description                              |
+=============================================+=============================+==========================================+
| count                                       | UINT32                      | number of selection structures           |
|                                             |                             |                                          |
|                                             |                             | A value of zero is allowed.              |
+---------------------------------------------+-----------------------------+------------------------------------------+
| pcrSelections[count]{:HASH_COUNT}           | [TPMS_PCR_SELECTION](       | list of selections                       |
|                                             | #sec:TPMS-PCR-SELECTION)    |                                          |
+---------------------------------------------+-----------------------------+------------------------------------------+
| #TPM_RC_SIZE                                |                             | response code when *count* is greater    |
|                                             |                             | than the possible number of banks        |
+---------------------------------------------+-----------------------------+------------------------------------------+


### TPML_ALG_PROPERTY {#sec:TPML-ALG-PROPERTY}

### TPML_ECC_CURVE {#sec:TPML-ECC-CURVE}

|                                                  | #sec:TPM-ECC-CURVE)        |                                      |

## Capabilities Structures

It is required that each parameter in the union described in @tbl:placeholder-table-127
be a list (TPML).

The number of returned elements in each list is determined by the size
of each list element and the maximum size set by the vendor as the
capability buffer (`MAX_CAP_BUFFER` in `TPM_PT_MAX_CAP_BUFFER`). The
maximum number of bytes in a list is:

`MAX_CAP_DATA` = `MAX_CAP_BUFFER` - sizeof(`TPM_CAP`) - sizeof(UINT32)

The maximum number of entries is then the number of complete list
elements that will fit in `MAX_CAP_DATA`.

::: Example ::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::
For a 1024-octet `MAX_CAP_BUFFER` a response containing a
`TPML_HANDLE` could have (1024 - 4 - 4) / 4 = 254 handles.
::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::

::: Note :::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::
`TPML_VENDOR_PROPERTY` was added in version 1.83. Older TPMs can
return a single `TPMS_TAGGED_PROPERTY` without a count.
::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::

## Clock/Counter Structures

### TPMS_CLOCK_INFO {#sec:TPMS-CLOCK-INFO}

This @tbl:placeholder-table-131 structure is used in each of the attestation commands.

Table: Definition of TPMS_CLOCK_INFO Structure {#tbl:placeholder-table-131}

+---------------------------+----------------------+-------------------------------------------------------------------+
| Parameter                 | Type                 | Description                                                       |
+===========================+======================+===================================================================+
| clock                     | UINT64               | time value in milliseconds that advances while the TPM is powered |
|                           |                      |                                                                   |
|                           |                      | ::: Note :::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::: |
|                           |                      | The interpretation of the time-origin (*clock*=0) is out of       |
|                           |                      | the scope of this specification, although Coordinated Universal   |
|                           |                      | Time (UTC) is expected to be a common convention. This structure  |
|                           |                      | element is used to report on the TPM's Clock value.               |
|                           |                      | ::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::: |
|                           |                      |                                                                   |
|                           |                      | This value is reset to zero when the Storage Primary Seed is      |
|                           |                      | changed (TPM2_Clear()).                                           |
|                           |                      |                                                                   |
|                           |                      | This value may be advanced by TPM2_ClockSet().                    |
+---------------------------+----------------------+-------------------------------------------------------------------+
| resetCount                | UINT32               | number of occurrences of TPM Reset since the last TPM2_Clear()    |
+---------------------------+----------------------+-------------------------------------------------------------------+
| restartCount              | UINT32               | number of times that TPM2_Shutdown() or _TPM_Hash_Start have      |
|                           |                      | occurred since the last TPM Reset or TPM2_Clear().                |
+---------------------------+----------------------+-------------------------------------------------------------------+
| safe                      | [TPMI_YES_NO](       | no value of *Clock* greater than the current value of             |
|                           | #sec:TPMI-YES-NO)    | *Clock* has been previously reported by the TPM. Set to YES on    |
|                           |                      | TPM2_Clear().                                                     |
+---------------------------+----------------------+-------------------------------------------------------------------+


### *Clock*

*Clock* is a monotonically increasing counter that advances whenever
power is applied to the TPM. The value of *Clock* may be set forward
with `TPM2_ClockSet()` if Owner Authorization or Platform Authorization
is provided. The value of *Clock* is incremented each millisecond.

`TPM2_Clear()` will set *Clock* to zero.

*Clock* will be non-volatile but may have a volatile component that is
updated every millisecond with the non-volatile component updated at a
lower rate. The reference for the millisecond timer is the TPM
oscillator. If the implementation uses a volatile component, the
non-volatile component shall be updated no less frequently than every
$2^{22}$ milliseconds (~69.9 minutes). The update rate of the
non-volatile portion of *Clock* shall be reported by a
`TPM2_GetCapability`(*capability* == `TPM_CAP_TPM_PROPERTIES`,
*property* == `TPM_PT_CLOCK_UPDATE`).

### *ResetCount*

This counter shall increment on each TPM Reset. This counter shall be
reset to zero by `TPM2_Clear()`.

### *RestartCount*

This counter shall increment by one for each TPM Restart or TPM Resume.
The *restartCount* shall be reset to zero on a TPM Reset or
`TPM2_Clear()`.

### *Safe*

This parameter is set to YES when the value reported in *Clock* is
guaranteed to be greater than any previous value for the current Owner.
It is set to NO when the value of *Clock* may have been reported in a
previous attestation or access.

::: Example ::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::
If *Safe* was NO at `TPM2_Shutdown()` and *Clock* does not
update unless a command is received, *Safe* will be NO if a
`TPM2_Startup()` was preceded by `TPM2_Shutdown()` with no intervening
commands. If *Clock* updates independent of commands, the non-volatile
bits of *Clock* can be updated, so *Safe* can be YES at `TPM2_Startup()`.

This parameter will be YES after the non-volatile bits of
*Clock* have been updated at the end of an update interval.
::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::

If a TPM implementation does not implement *Clock*, *Safe* shall always
be NO and `TPMS_CLOCK_INFO`.*clock* shall always be zero.

This parameter will be set to YES by `TPM2_Clear()`.

### TPMS_TIME_INFO {#sec:TPMS-TIME-INFO}

This @tbl:placeholder-table-132 structure is used in, e.g., the `TPM2_GetTime()`
attestation and `TPM2_ReadClock()`.

The *Time* value reported in this structure is reset whenever power to
the *Time* circuit is reestablished. If required, an implementation may
reset the value of *Time* any time before the TPM returns after
`TPM2_Startup()`. The value of *Time* shall increment continuously while
power is applied to the TPM.

Table: Definition of TPMS_TIME_INFO Structure {#tbl:placeholder-table-132}

+-----------------------+--------------------------+-------------------------------------------------------------------+
| Parameter             | Type                     | Description                                                       |
+=======================+==========================+===================================================================+
| time                  | UINT64                   | time in milliseconds since the *Time* circuit was last reset      |
|                       |                          |                                                                   |
|                       |                          | This structure element is used to report on the TPM's *Time*      |
|                       |                          | value.                                                            |
+-----------------------+--------------------------+-------------------------------------------------------------------+
| clockInfo             | [TPMS_CLOCK_INFO](       | a structure containing the clock information                      |
|                       | #sec:TPMS-CLOCK-INFO)    |                                                                   |
+-----------------------+--------------------------+-------------------------------------------------------------------+


## TPM Attestation Structures {#sec:tpm-attestation-structures}

### Introduction

@sec:tpm-attestation-structures describes the structures that are used when a TPM creates a
structure to be signed. The signing structures follow a standard format
`TPM2B_ATTEST` with case-specific information embedded.

### TPMS_TIME_ATTEST_INFO {#sec:TPMS-TIME-ATTEST-INFO}

This @tbl:placeholder-table-133 structure is used when the TPM performs `TPM2_GetTime()`.

Table: Definition of TPMS_TIME_ATTEST_INFO Structure {#tbl:placeholder-table-133}

+----------------------+-----------------------------+-----------------------------------------------------------------+
| Parameter            | Type                        | Description                                                     |
+======================+=============================+=================================================================+
| time                 | [TPMS_TIME_INFO](           | the *Time*, *Clock*, *resetCount*,                              |
|                      | #sec:TPMS-TIME-INFO)        | *restartCount*, and *Safe* indicator                            |
+----------------------+-----------------------------+-----------------------------------------------------------------+
| firmwareVersion      | UINT64                      | a TPM vendor-specific value indicating the version number of    |
|                      |                             | the firmware                                                    |
+----------------------+-----------------------------+-----------------------------------------------------------------+

### TPMU_ATTEST {#sec:TPMU-ATTEST}

@tbl:placeholder-table-142 defines the TPMU_ATTEST Structure.

Table: Definition of TPMU_ATTEST Union  {#tbl:placeholder-table-142}

+-------------------+-----------------------------------------+--------------------------------------------------------+
| Parameter         | Type                                    | Selector                                               |
+===================+=========================================+========================================================+
| certify           | [TPMS_CERTIFY_INFO](                    | TPM_ST_ATTEST_CERTIFY                                  |
|                   | #sec:TPMS-CERTIFY-INFO)                 |                                                        |
+-------------------+-----------------------------------------+--------------------------------------------------------+
| creation          | [TPMS_CREATION_INFO](                   | TPM_ST_ATTEST_CREATION                                 |
|                   | #sec:TPMS-CREATION-INFO)                |                                                        |
+-------------------+-----------------------------------------+--------------------------------------------------------+
| quote             | [TPMS_QUOTE_INFO](                      | TPM_ST_ATTEST_QUOTE                                    |
|                   | #sec:TPMS-QUOTE-INFO)                   |                                                        |
+-------------------+-----------------------------------------+--------------------------------------------------------+
| commandAudit      | [TPMS_COMMAND_AUDIT_INFO](              | TPM_ST_ATTEST_COMMAND_AUDIT                            |
|                   | #sec:TPMS-COMMAND-AUDIT-INFO)           |                                                        |
+-------------------+-----------------------------------------+--------------------------------------------------------+
| sessionAudit      | [TPMS_SESSION_AUDIT_INFO](              | TPM_ST_ATTEST_SESSION_AUDIT                            |
|                   | #sec:TPMS-SESSION-AUDIT-INFO)           |                                                        |
+-------------------+-----------------------------------------+--------------------------------------------------------+
