+-------------------+-----------------------------------------+--------------------------------------------------------+


### TPMS_ATTEST {#sec:TPMS-ATTEST}

This @tbl:placeholder-table-143 structure is used on each TPM-generated signed structure.
The signature is over this structure.

When the structure is signed by a key in the Storage hierarchy, the
values of *clockInfo.resetCount*, *clockInfo.restartCount*, and
*firmwareVersion* are obfuscated with a per-key obfuscation value.

Table: Definition of TPMS_ATTEST Structure  {#tbl:placeholder-table-143}

+-----------------------------+-----------------------------+----------------------------------------------------------+
| Parameter                   | Type                        | Description                                              |
+=============================+=============================+==========================================================+
| magic                       | [TPM_CONSTANTS32](          | the indication that this structure was created by a TPM  |
|                             | #sec:TPM-CONSTANTS32)       | (always TPM_GENERATED_VALUE)                             |
+-----------------------------+-----------------------------+----------------------------------------------------------+
| type                        | [TPMI_ST_ATTEST](           | type of the attestation structure                        |
|                             | #sec:TPMI-ST-ATTEST)        |                                                          |
+-----------------------------+-----------------------------+----------------------------------------------------------+
| qualifiedSigner             | [TPM2B_NAME](               | Qualified Name of the signing key                        |
|                             | #sec:TPM2B-NAME)            |                                                          |
+-----------------------------+-----------------------------+----------------------------------------------------------+
| extraData                   | [TPM2B_DATA](               | external information supplied by caller                  |
|                             | #sec:TPM2B-DATA)            |                                                          |
|                             |                             | ::: Note ::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::: |
|                             |                             | A TPM2B_DATA structure provides room for a digest        |
|                             |                             | and a method indicator to indicate the components of the |
|                             |                             | digest. The definition of this method indicator is       |
|                             |                             | outside the scope of this specification.                 |
|                             |                             | :::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::: |
+-----------------------------+-----------------------------+----------------------------------------------------------+
| clockInfo                   | [TPMS_CLOCK_INFO](          | Clock, resetCount, restartCount, and Safe                |
|                             | #sec:TPMS-CLOCK-INFO)       |                                                          |
+-----------------------------+-----------------------------+----------------------------------------------------------+
| firmwareVersion             | UINT64                      | TPM-vendor-specific value identifying the version number |
|                             |                             | of the firmware                                          |
+-----------------------------+-----------------------------+----------------------------------------------------------+
| \[type\]attested            | [TPMU_ATTEST](              | the type-specific attestation information                |
|                             | #sec:TPMU-ATTEST)           |                                                          |
+-----------------------------+-----------------------------+----------------------------------------------------------+


### TPM2B_ATTEST {#sec:TPM2B-ATTEST}

This @tbl:placeholder-table-144 sized buffer contains the signed structure. The
*attestationData* is the signed portion of the structure. The *size*
parameter is not signed.

Table: Definition of TPM2B_ATTEST Structure  {#tbl:placeholder-table-144}

+--------------------------------------------------+-----------------------+-------------------------------------------+
| Parameter                                        | Type                  | Description                               |
+==================================================+=======================+===========================================+
| size                                             | UINT16                | size of the *attestationData* structure   |
+--------------------------------------------------+-----------------------+-------------------------------------------+
| attestationData\[size\]{:sizeof([TPMS_ATTEST](   | BYTE                  | the signed structure                      |
| #sec:TPMS-ATTEST))}                              |                       |                                           |
+--------------------------------------------------+-----------------------+-------------------------------------------+


## Authorization Structures {#sec:authorization-structures}

### Introduction

The structures in @sec:authorization-structures are used for all authorizations. One or more of
these structures will be present in a command or response that has a tag
of `TPM_ST_SESSIONS`.

### TPMS_AUTH_COMMAND {#sec:TPMS-AUTH-COMMAND}

This @tbl:placeholder-table-144 structure is the format used for each of the
authorizations in the session area of a command.

Table: Definition of TPMS_AUTH_COMMAND Structure  {#tbl:placeholder-table-145}

+------------------------+---------------------------------+-----------------------------------------------------------+
| Parameter              | Type                            | Description                                               |
+========================+=================================+===========================================================+
| sessionHandle          | [TPMI_SH_AUTH_SESSION](         | the session handle                                        |
|                        | #sec:TPMI-SH-AUTH-SESSION)+     |                                                           |
+------------------------+---------------------------------+-----------------------------------------------------------+
| nonce                  | [TPM2B_NONCE](                  | the session nonce, may be the Empty Buffer                |
|                        | #sec:TPM2B-NONCE)               |                                                           |
+------------------------+---------------------------------+-----------------------------------------------------------+
| sessionAttributes      | [TPMA_SESSION](                 | the session attributes                                    |
|                        | #sec:TPMA-SESSION)              |                                                           |
+------------------------+---------------------------------+-----------------------------------------------------------+
| hmac                   | [TPM2B_AUTH](                   | either an HMAC, a password, or an EmptyAuth               |
|                        | #sec:TPM2B-AUTH)                |                                                           |
+------------------------+---------------------------------+-----------------------------------------------------------+


### TPMS_AUTH_RESPONSE {#sec:TPMS-AUTH-RESPONSE}

This @tbl:placeholder-table-146 structure is the format for each of the authorizations in
the session area of the response. If the TPM returns `TPM_RC_SUCCESS`,
then the session area of the response contains the same number of
authorizations as the command and the authorizations are in the same
order.

Table: Definition of TPMS_AUTH_RESPONSE Structure  {#tbl:placeholder-table-146}

+------------------------+---------------------------------+-----------------------------------------------------------+
| Parameter              | Type                            | Description                                               |
+========================+=================================+===========================================================+
| nonce                  | [TPM2B_NONCE](                  | the session nonce, may be the Empty Buffer                |
|                        | #sec:TPM2B-NONCE)               |                                                           |
+------------------------+---------------------------------+-----------------------------------------------------------+
| sessionAttributes      | [TPMA_SESSION](                 | the session attributes                                    |
|                        | #sec:TPMA-SESSION)              |                                                           |
+------------------------+---------------------------------+-----------------------------------------------------------+
| hmac                   | [TPM2B_AUTH](                   | either an HMAC or an EmptyAuth                            |
|                        | #sec:TPM2B-AUTH)                |                                                           |
+------------------------+---------------------------------+-----------------------------------------------------------+


# Algorithm Parameters and Structures

## Symmetric

### Introduction

This clause defines the parameters and structures for describing
symmetric algorithms.

### Symmetric Cipher Key Sizes

This @tbl:sym-cipher-key-sizes interface type defines the supported key sizes
for each symmetric algorithm. This type is used to allow an unmarshaling
routine to generate the proper validation code (`TPM_RC_VALUE`) for an
unsupported key sizes. An implementation that supports different key sizes
would have a different set of selections.

::: Note :::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::
Key size is expressed in bits.
::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::

Table: Definition of Types for Symmetric Cipher Key Sizes {#tbl:sym-cipher-key-sizes}

+------------------------------+---------------------------------------+---------------------------+
| Type                         | Name                                  | Allowed Values            |
+==============================+=======================================+===========================+
| [TPM_KEY_BITS](              | TPMI_TDES_KEY_BITS                    | {128, 192}                |
| #sec:TPM-KEY-BITS)           |                                       |                           |
|                              | ::: Deprecated :::::::::::::::::::::: |                           |
|                              | TDES is deprecated. See Part 0.       |                           |
|                              | ::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::: |                           |
+------------------------------+---------------------------------------+---------------------------+
| [TPM_KEY_BITS](              | TPMI_AES_KEY_BITS                     | {128, 192, 256}           |
| #sec:TPM-KEY-BITS)           |                                       |                           |
+------------------------------+---------------------------------------+---------------------------+
| [TPM_KEY_BITS](              | TPMI_SM4_KEY_BITS                     | {128}                     |
| #sec:TPM-KEY-BITS)           |                                       |                           |
+------------------------------+---------------------------------------+---------------------------+
| [TPM_KEY_BITS](              | TPMI_CAMELLIA_KEY_BITS                | {128, 192, 256}           |
| #sec:TPM-KEY-BITS)           |                                       |                           |
+------------------------------+---------------------------------------+---------------------------+

### TPMU_SYM_KEY_BITS {#sec:TPMU-SYM-KEY-BITS}

This @tbl:placeholder-table-148 union is used to collect the symmetric encryption key
sizes.

The *xor* entry is a hash algorithms selector and not a key size in
bits. This overload is used in order to avoid an additional level of
indirection with another union and another set of selectors.

The *xor* entry is only selected in a `TPMT_SYM_DEF`, which is used to
select the parameter encryption value.

<!-- TODO: https://github.com/TrustedComputingGroup/TPM-Spec/issues/200 

Consider defining separate TPMUs for the TPMT_SYM_DEF and TPMT_SYM_DEF_OBJECT
cases, so that "whether *xor* is there" is part of the definition of the
structure instead of the text above. -->

Table: Definition of TPMU_SYM_KEY_BITS Union {#tbl:placeholder-table-148}

+----------------------+------------------------------------+----------------------------+-----------------------------+
| Parameter            | Type                               | Selector                   | Description                 |
+======================+====================================+============================+=============================+
| tdes                 | [TPMI_TDES_KEY_BITS](              | TPM_ALG_TDES               | ::: Deprecated :::::::::::: |
|                      | #tbl:sym-cipher-key-sizes)         |                            | TDES is deprecated. See     |
|                      |                                    |                            | Part 0.                     |
|                      |                                    |                            | ::::::::::::::::::::::::::: |
+----------------------+------------------------------------+----------------------------+-----------------------------+
| aes                  | [TPMI_AES_KEY_BITS](               | TPM_ALG_AES                |                             |
|                      | #tbl:sym-cipher-key-sizes)         |                            |                             |
+----------------------+------------------------------------+----------------------------+-----------------------------+
| xor                  | [TPMI_ALG_HASH](                   | TPM_ALG_XOR                | overload for using *xor*    |
|                      | #sec:TPMI-ALG-HASH)                |                            |                             |
|                      |                                    |                            | ::: Note :::::::::::::::::: |
|                      |                                    |                            | TPM_ALG_NULL is not allowed.|
|                      |                                    |                            | ::::::::::::::::::::::::::: |
+----------------------+------------------------------------+----------------------------+-----------------------------+
| sm4                  | [TPMI_SM4_KEY_BITS](               | TPM_ALG_SM4                |                             |
|                      | #tbl:sym-cipher-key-sizes)         |                            |                             |
+----------------------+------------------------------------+----------------------------+-----------------------------+
| camellia             | [TPMI_CAMELLIA_KEY_BITS](          | TPM_ALG_CAMELLIA           |                             |
|                      | #tbl:sym-cipher-key-sizes)         |                            |                             |
+----------------------+------------------------------------+----------------------------+-----------------------------+
| null                 |                                    | TPM_ALG_NULL               |                             |
+----------------------+------------------------------------+----------------------------+-----------------------------+

### TPMT_SYM_DEF {#sec:TPMT-SYM-DEF}

The @tbl:placeholder-table-151 TPMT_SYM_DEF Structure is used to select an algorithm to
be used for parameter encryption in those cases when different symmetric
algorithms may be selected.

+-------------------------+-----------------------------------+--------------------------------------------------------+

+-------------------------+-----------------------------------+--------------------------------------------------------+
| \[algorithm\]mode       | [TPMU_SYM_MODE](                  | the mode for the key                                   |

+-------------------------+-----------------------------------+--------------------------------------------------------+

|                         | #sec:TPMU-SYM-DETAILS)            |                                                        |
+-------------------------+-----------------------------------+--------------------------------------------------------+


### TPMT_SYM_DEF_OBJECT {#sec:TPMT-SYM-DEF-OBJECT}

This @tbl:placeholder-table-152 structure is used when different symmetric block cipher
(not XOR) algorithms may be selected.

<!-- If the Object can be an ordinary
parent (not a derivation parent), this must be the first field in the
Object's parameter (see @sec:TPMU-PUBLIC-PARMS) field. -->

|                         |                                   | this shall be a supported block cipher and not         |
|                         |                                   | TPM_ALG_NULL                                           |
+-------------------------+-----------------------------------+--------------------------------------------------------+
| \[algorithm\]keyBits    | [TPMU_SYM_KEY_BITS](              | the key size                                           |

+-------------------------+-----------------------------------+--------------------------------------------------------+


### TPM2B_SYM_KEY {#sec:TPM2B-SYM-KEY}

This @tbl:placeholder-table-153 structure is used to hold a symmetric key in the
sensitive area of an asymmetric object.

The number of bits in the key is in *keyBits* in the public area. When
*keyBits* is not an even multiple of 8 bits, the unused bits of *buffer*
will be the most significant bits of *buffer*\[0\] and *size* will be
rounded up to the number of octets required to hold all bits of the key.

::: Note :::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::
`MAX_SYM_KEY_BYTES` will be the larger of the largest symmetric
key supported by the TPM and the largest digest produced by any hashing
algorithm implemented on the TPM.
::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::

Table: Definition of TPM2B_SYM_KEY Structure {#tbl:placeholder-table-153}

+-------------------------------------------+----------------+---------------------------------------------------------+
| Parameter                                 | Type           | Description                                             |
+===========================================+================+=========================================================+
| size                                      | UINT16         | size, in octets, of the buffer containing the key; may  |
|                                           |                | be zero                                                 |
+-------------------------------------------+----------------+---------------------------------------------------------+
| buffer \[size\] {:MAX_SYM_KEY_BYTES}      | BYTE           | the key                                                 |
+-------------------------------------------+----------------+---------------------------------------------------------+


### TPMS_SYMCIPHER_PARMS {#sec:TPMS-SYMCIPHER-PARMS}

This @tbl:placeholder-table-154 structure contains the parameters for a symmetric block
cipher object.

Table: Definition of TPMS_SYMCIPHER_PARMS Structure {#tbl:placeholder-table-154}

+---------------------------+-------------------------------+----------------------------------------------------------+
| Parameter                 | Type                          | Description                                              |
+===========================+===============================+==========================================================+
| sym                       | [TPMT_SYM_DEF_OBJECT](        | a symmetric block cipher                                 |
|                           | #sec:TPMT-SYM-DEF-OBJECT)     |                                                          |
+---------------------------+-------------------------------+----------------------------------------------------------+


### TPM2B_LABEL {#sec:TPM2B-LABEL}

This @tbl:placeholder-table-155 buffer holds a *label* or *context* value. For
interoperability and backwards compatibility, `LABEL_MAX_BUFFER` is the
minimum of the largest digest on the device and the largest ECC
parameter (`MAX_ECC_KEY_BYTES`) but no more than 32 bytes.

All implementations are required to support at least one hash algorithm
that produces a digest of 32 bytes or larger; and any implementation
that supports ECC is required to support at least one curve with a key
size of 32-bytes or larger.

::: Note :::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::
Although the maximum size allowed for a *label* or *context* is 32
bytes, the object data structure needs to be sized to allow a 32-byte
value.
::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::

Table: Definition of TPM2B_LABEL Structure {#tbl:placeholder-table-155}

+--------------------------------------------+----------------------------+--------------------------------------------+
| Parameter                                  | Type                       | Description                                |
+============================================+============================+============================================+
| size                                       | UINT16                     |                                            |
+--------------------------------------------+----------------------------+--------------------------------------------+
| buffer\[size\]{:LABEL_MAX_BUFFER}          | BYTE                       | symmetric data for a created object or the |
|                                            |                            | *label* and                                |
|                                            |                            | *context* for a derived object             |
+--------------------------------------------+----------------------------+--------------------------------------------+


### TPMS_DERIVE {#sec:TPMS-DERIVE}

This @tbl:placeholder-table-156 structure contains the *label* and *context* fields for a
derived object. These values are used in the derivation KDF. The values
in the *unique* field of *inPublic* area template take precedence over
the values in the *inSensitive* parameter.

Table: Definition of TPMS_DERIVE Structure {#tbl:placeholder-table-156}

+--------------------------------------------+----------------------------+--------------------------------------------+
| Parameter                                  | Type                       | Description                                |
+============================================+============================+============================================+
| label                                      | [TPM2B_LABEL](             |                                            |
|                                            | #sec:TPM2B-LABEL)          |                                            |
+--------------------------------------------+----------------------------+--------------------------------------------+
| context                                    | [TPM2B_LABEL](             |                                            |
|                                            | #sec:TPM2B-LABEL)          |                                            |
+--------------------------------------------+----------------------------+--------------------------------------------+


### TPM2B_DERIVE {#sec:TPM2B-DERIVE}

@tbl:placeholder-table-157 structure defines the TPM2B_DERIVE Structure.

Table: Definition of TPM2B_DERIVE Structure {#tbl:placeholder-table-157}

+--------------------------------------------+----------------------------+--------------------------------------------+
| Parameter                                  | Type                       | Description                                |
+============================================+============================+============================================+
| size                                       | UINT16                     |                                            |
+--------------------------------------------+----------------------------+--------------------------------------------+
| buffer\[size\]{: sizeof([TPMS_DERIVE](     | BYTE                       | symmetric data for a created object or the |
| #sec:TPMS-DERIVE))}                        |                            | *label* and                                |
|                                            |                            | *context* for a derived object             |
+--------------------------------------------+----------------------------+--------------------------------------------+


### TPMU_SENSITIVE_CREATE {#sec:TPMU-SENSITIVE-CREATE}

This @tbl:placeholder-table-158 structure allows a TPM2B_SENSITIVE_CREATE structure to
carry either a TPM2B_SENSITIVE_DATA or a TPM2B_DERIVE Structure. The
contents of the union are determined by context. When an object is being
derived, the derivation values are present.

For interoperability, `MAX_SYM_DATA` should be 128.

Table: Definition of TPMU_SENSITIVE_CREATE Union {#tbl:placeholder-table-158}

+------------------------------+------------------------+------------------------+-------------------------------------+
| Parameter                    | Type                   | Selector               | Description                         |
+==============================+========================+========================+=====================================+
| create\[MAX_SYM_DATA\]       | BYTE                   |                        | sensitive data for a created        |
|                              |                        |                        | symmetric Object                    |
+------------------------------+------------------------+------------------------+-------------------------------------+
| derive                       | [TPMS_DERIVE](         |                        | *label* and *context* for a derived |
|                              | #sec:TPMS-DERIVE)      |                        | Object                              |
+------------------------------+------------------------+------------------------+-------------------------------------+


### TPM2B_SENSITIVE_DATA {#sec:TPM2B-SENSITIVE-DATA}

This @tbl:placeholder-table-159 buffer wraps the TPMU_SENSITIVE_CREATE Structure.

Table: Definition of TPM2B_SENSITIVE_DATA Structure {#tbl:placeholder-table-159}

+-------------------------------------------------------+-----------------+--------------------------------------------+
| Parameter                                             | Type            | Description                                |
+=======================================================+=================+============================================+
| size                                                  | UINT16          |                                            |
+-------------------------------------------------------+-----------------+--------------------------------------------+
| buffer\[size\]{: sizeof([TPMU_SENSITIVE_CREATE](      | BYTE            | symmetric data for a created object or the |
| #sec:TPMU-SENSITIVE-CREATE))}                         |                 | *label* and                                |
|                                                       |                 | *context* for a derived object             |
+-------------------------------------------------------+-----------------+--------------------------------------------+


### TPMS_SENSITIVE_CREATE {#sec:TPMS-SENSITIVE-CREATE}

This @tbl:placeholder-table-160 structure defines the values to be placed in the
sensitive area of a created object. This structure is only used within a
TPM2B_SENSITIVE_CREATE Structure.

::: Note :::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::
When sent to the TPM or unsealed, data is usually encrypted using
parameter encryption.
::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::

If *data.size* is not zero, and the object is not a *keyedHash*,
*data.size* must match the size indicated in the *keySize* *o*f
*public.parameters.* If the object is a *keyedHash*, *data*.*size* may
be any value up to the maximum allowed in a `TPM2B_SENSITIVE_DATA`.

For an asymmetric object, data shall be an Empty Buffer and
*sensitiveDataOrigin* shall be SET.

Table: Definition of TPMS_SENSITIVE_CREATE Structure  {#tbl:placeholder-table-160}

+----------------------+------------------------------------+----------------------------------------------------------+
| Parameter            | Type                               | Description                                              |
+======================+====================================+==========================================================+
| userAuth             | [TPM2B_AUTH](                      | the USER auth secret value                               |
|                      | #sec:TPM2B-AUTH)                   |                                                          |
+----------------------+------------------------------------+----------------------------------------------------------+
| data                 | [TPM2B_SENSITIVE_DATA](            | data to be sealed, a key, or derivation values           |
|                      | #sec:TPM2B-SENSITIVE-DATA)         |                                                          |
+----------------------+------------------------------------+----------------------------------------------------------+


### TPM2B_SENSITIVE_CREATE {#sec:TPM2B-SENSITIVE-CREATE}

This @tbl:placeholder-table-161 structure contains the sensitive creation data in a sized
buffer. This structure is defined so that both the *userAuth* and *data*
values of the `TPMS_SENSITIVE_CREATE` may be passed as a single
parameter for parameter encryption purposes.

Table: Definition of TPM2B_SENSITIVE_CREATE Structure  {#tbl:placeholder-table-161}

+----------------------+------------------------------------+----------------------------------------------------------+
| Parameter            | Type                               | Description                                              |
+======================+====================================+==========================================================+
| size=                | UINT16                             | size of *sensitive* in octets (may not be zero)          |
|                      |                                    |                                                          |
|                      |                                    | ::: Note ::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::: |
|                      |                                    | The *userAuth* and data parameters in this buffer        |
|                      |                                    | can both be zero length but the minimum size of this     |
|                      |                                    | parameter will be the sum of the size fields of the two  |
|                      |                                    | parameters of the TPMS_SENSITIVE_CREATE.                 |
|                      |                                    | :::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::: |
+----------------------+------------------------------------+----------------------------------------------------------+
| sensitive            | [TPMS_SENSITIVE_CREATE](           | data to be sealed or a symmetric key value.              |
|                      | #sec:TPMS-SENSITIVE-CREATE)        |                                                          |
+----------------------+------------------------------------+----------------------------------------------------------+

## Asymmetric

### Signing Schemes

#### Introduction

These structures are used to define the method in which the signature is
to be created. These schemes would appear in an object's public area and
in commands where the signing scheme is variable.

Every scheme is required to indicate a hash that is used in digesting
the message.

#### RSA Signature Schemes

These are the @tbl:tpms-sig-schemes-rsa RSA schemes that only need a hash algorithm as a
scheme parameter.

For the `TPM_ALG_RSAPSS` signing scheme, the same hash algorithm is used
for digesting TPM-generated data (an attestation structure) and in the
KDF used for the masking operation. The salt size is always the largest
salt value that will fit into the available space.

Table: Definition of (RSA) Types for RSA Signature Schemes {#tbl:tpms-sig-schemes-rsa}

+-------------------------------+----------------------------------------------+
| Type                          | Name                                         |
+===============================+==============================================+
| [TPMS_SCHEME_HASH](           | TPMS_SIG_SCHEME_RSASSA                       |
| #sec:TPMS-SCHEME-HASH)        |                                              |
+-------------------------------+----------------------------------------------+
| [TPMS_SCHEME_HASH](           | TPMS_SIG_SCHEME_RSAPSS                       |
| #sec:TPMS-SCHEME-HASH)        |                                              |
+-------------------------------+----------------------------------------------+


#### ECC Signature Schemes

Most of the @tbl:tpms-sig-schemes-ecc ECC signature schemes only require a hash
algorithm to complete the definition and can be typed as
`TPMS_SCHEME_HASH`. Anonymous algorithms also require a count value so
they are typed to be `TPMS_SCHEME_ECDAA`.

Table: Definition of (ECC) Types for ECC Signature Schemes {#tbl:tpms-sig-schemes-ecc}

+-------------------------------+----------------------------------------------+
| Type                          | Name                                         |
+===============================+==============================================+
| [TPMS_SCHEME_HASH](           | TPMS_SIG_SCHEME_ECDSA                        |
| #sec:TPMS-SCHEME-HASH)        |                                              |
+-------------------------------+----------------------------------------------+
| [TPMS_SCHEME_ECDAA](          | TPMS_SIG_SCHEME_ECDAA                        |
| #sec:TPMS-SCHEME-ECDAA)       |                                              |
+-------------------------------+----------------------------------------------+
| [TPMS_SCHEME_HASH](           | TPMS_SIG_SCHEME_SM2                          |
| #sec:TPMS-SCHEME-HASH)        |                                              |
+-------------------------------+----------------------------------------------+
| [TPMS_SCHEME_HASH](           | TPMS_SIG_SCHEME_ECSCHNORR                    |
| #sec:TPMS-SCHEME-HASH)        |                                              |
+-------------------------------+----------------------------------------------+
| [TPMS_EMPTY](                 | TPMS_SIG_SCHEME_EDDSA                        |
| #sec:TPMS-EMPTY)              |                                              |
+-------------------------------+----------------------------------------------+
| [TPMS_EMPTY](                 | TPMS_SIG_SCHEME_HASH_EDDSA                   |
| #sec:TPMS-EMPTY)              |                                              |
+-------------------------------+----------------------------------------------+

::: Note :::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::
The SM2 Digital Signature Algorithm does not specify a specific cryptographic
hash algorithm. The TPM does not automatically provide a default; if
`TPMS_SIG_SCHEME_SM2.hashAlg` is `TPM_ALG_NULL`, the TPM will return an error.
However, per section 5.4.2 of @{gb32918-2}, this value should usually be
SM3 (`TPM_ALG_SM3_256`).
::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::

### Encryption Schemes

#### Introduction

These structures are used to indicate the algorithm used for the
encrypting process. These schemes would appear in an object's public
area.

::: Note :::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::
With ECC, the only encryption is with a key exchange of a symmetric
key or seed.
::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::

### Key Derivation Schemes

#### Introduction

These @tbl:kdf-schemes structures are used to define the key derivation for
symmetric secret sharing using asymmetric methods. A secret sharing
scheme is required in any asymmetric key with the *decrypt* attribute
SET.

These schemes would appear in an object's public area and in commands
where the secret sharing scheme is variable.

Each scheme includes a symmetric algorithm and a KDF selection.

The qualifying value for each of the KDF schemes is the hash algorithm.

::: Note :::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::
These types do not appear in the Reference Code in the
specification but are used in the unmarshaling code.
::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::

Table: Definition of Types for KDF Schemes {#tbl:kdf-schemes}

+------------------------------------+-----------------------------------------+
| Type                               | Name                                    |
+====================================+=========================================+
| [TPMS_SCHEME_HASH](                | TPMS_KDF_SCHEME_MGF1                    |
| #sec:TPMS-SCHEME-HASH)             |                                         |
+------------------------------------+-----------------------------------------+
| [TPMS_SCHEME_HASH](                | TPMS_KDF_SCHEME_KDF2                    |
| #sec:TPMS-SCHEME-HASH)             |                                         |
+------------------------------------+-----------------------------------------+
| [TPMS_SCHEME_HASH](                | TPMS_KDF_SCHEME_KDF1_SP800_56A          |
| #sec:TPMS-SCHEME-HASH)             |                                         |
+------------------------------------+-----------------------------------------+
| [TPMS_SCHEME_HASH](                | TPMS_KDF_SCHEME_KDF1_SP800_108          |
| #sec:TPMS-SCHEME-HASH)             |                                         |
+------------------------------------+-----------------------------------------+
| [TPMS_SCHEME_HASH](                | TPMS_KDF_SCHEME_HKDF                    |
| #sec:TPMS-SCHEME-HASH)             |                                         |
+------------------------------------+-----------------------------------------+

#### TPM2B_PUBLIC_KEY_RSA {#sec:TPM2B-PUBLIC-KEY-RSA}

This @tbl:placeholder-table-185 sized buffer holds the largest RSA public key supported
by the TPM.

Table: Definition of (RSA) TPM2B_PUBLIC_KEY_RSA Structure {#tbl:placeholder-table-185}

+----------------------------------------------+-----------------+-----------------------------------------------------+
| Parameter                                    | Type            | Description                                         |
+==============================================+=================+=====================================================+
| size                                         | UINT16          | size of the buffer                                  |
|                                              |                 |                                                     |
|                                              |                 | The value of zero is only valid for create.         |
+----------------------------------------------+-----------------+-----------------------------------------------------+
| buffer\[size\] {: MAX_RSA_KEY_BYTES}         | BYTE            | value                                               |
+----------------------------------------------+-----------------+-----------------------------------------------------+


#### TPMI_RSA_KEY_BITS {#sec:TPMI-RSA-KEY-BITS}

This @tbl:placeholder-table-186 holds the value that is the maximum size allowed for an
RSA key.

::: Note :::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::
An implementation is allowed to provide limited support for
smaller RSA key sizes. That is, a TPM may be able to accept a smaller
RSA key size in `TPM2_LoadExternal()` when only the public area is loaded
but not accept that smaller key size in any command that loads both the
public and private portions of an RSA key. This would allow the TPM to
validate signatures using the smaller key but would prevent the TPM from
using the smaller key size for any other purpose.
::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::

Table: Definition of (RSA) (TPM_KEY_BITS) TPMI_RSA_KEY_BITS Type {#tbl:placeholder-table-186}

+----------------------------------+-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
| Parameter                        | Description                                                                       |
+==================================+===================================================================================+
| $RSA_KEY_SIZES_BITS              | the number of bits in the supported key                                           |
+----------------------------------+-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------+
| #TPM_RC_VALUE                    | error when key size is not supported                                              |
+----------------------------------+-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------+


#### TPM2B_PRIVATE_KEY_RSA {#sec:TPM2B-PRIVATE-KEY-RSA}

This @tbl:placeholder-table-187 sized buffer holds the largest RSA prime number supported
by the TPM.

::: Note :::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::
All primes are required to have exactly half the number of
significant bits as the public modulus, and the square of each prime is
required to have the same number of significant bits as the public
modulus.
::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::

::: Note :::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::
`RSA_PRIVATE_SIZE` is a vendor specific value that can be
(`MAX_RSA_KEY_BYTES` / 2) or ((`MAX_RSA_KEY_BYTES` \* 5) / 2). The
larger size would only apply to keys that have *fixedTPM* parents. The
larger size was added in version 1.59.
::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::

Table: Definition of (RSA) TPM2B_PRIVATE_KEY_RSA Structure {#tbl:placeholder-table-187}

+----------------------------------------------+-----------------+-----------------------------------------------------+
| Parameter                                    | Type            | Description                                         |
+==============================================+=================+=====================================================+
| size                                         | UINT16          |                                                     |
+----------------------------------------------+-----------------+-----------------------------------------------------+
| buffer\[size\]{:RSA_PRIVATE_SIZE }           | BYTE            |                                                     |
+----------------------------------------------+-----------------+-----------------------------------------------------+

#### TPMS_ECC_POINT {#sec:TPMS-ECC-POINT}

This @tbl:placeholder-table-189 structure holds two ECC coordinates that, together, make
up an ECC point.

*y* is zero-length for the Edwards curves (Curve25519 and Curve448). See
Part 1 for details on how ECC points are represented using this structure.

Table: Definition of (ECC) TPMS_ECC_POINT Structure {#tbl:placeholder-table-189}

+----------------------------------+---------------------------------+-------------------------------------------------+
| Parameter                        | Type                            | Description                                     |
+==================================+=================================+=================================================+
| x                                | [TPM2B_ECC_PARAMETER](          | X coordinate                                    |
|                                  | #sec:TPM2B-ECC-PARAMETER)       |                                                 |
+----------------------------------+---------------------------------+-------------------------------------------------+
| y                                | [TPM2B_ECC_PARAMETER](          | Y coordinate                                    |
|                                  | #sec:TPM2B-ECC-PARAMETER)       |                                                 |
+----------------------------------+---------------------------------+-------------------------------------------------+

#### TPM2B_ECC_POINT {#sec:TPM2B-ECC-POINT}

This @tbl:placeholder-table-190 structure is defined to allow a point to be a single
sized parameter so that it may be encrypted.

::: Note :::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::
If the point is to be omitted, the X and Y coordinates need to be
individually set to Empty Buffers. The minimum value for size will be
four. It is checked indirectly by unmarshaling of the `TPMS_ECC_POINT`.
If the type of *point* were BYTE, then *size* could have been zero.
However, this would complicate the process of marshaling the structure.
::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::

Table: Definition of (ECC) TPM2B_ECC_POINT Structure {#tbl:placeholder-table-190}

+----------------------------------+---------------------------------+-------------------------------------------------+
| Parameter                        | Type                            | Description                                     |
+==================================+=================================+=================================================+
| size=                            | UINT16                          | size of the remainder of this structure         |
+----------------------------------+---------------------------------+-------------------------------------------------+
| point                            | [TPMS_ECC_POINT](               | coordinates                                     |
|                                  | #sec:TPMS-ECC-POINT)            |                                                 |
+----------------------------------+---------------------------------+-------------------------------------------------+
| #TPM_RC_SIZE                     |                                 | error returned if the unmarshaled size of       |
|                                  |                                 | *point* is not exactly equal to *size*          |
+----------------------------------+---------------------------------+-------------------------------------------------+

### ML-KEM

#### TPMI_MLKEM_PARMS {#sec:TPMI-MLKEM-PARMS}

@tbl:tpmi-mlkem-parms enumerates the ML-KEM parameter sets that can be
implemented by a TPM.

Numeric values of TPM_MLKEM_x correspond to the numeric values of TCG_MLKEM_x
from the TCG Algorithm Registry [@{alg-registry}].

Public key, ciphertext, and shared secret key sizes correspond to the listed
sizes for each parameter set in FIPS 203 [@{fips203}].

Table: Definition of (MLKEM) TPMI_MLKEM_PARMS Type {#tbl:tpmi-mlkem-parms}

+-----------------------+-----------------+-----------------+-----------------+--------------------+
| Parameter set         | Numeric value   | Public key size | Ciphertext size | Shared secret      |
|                       |                 | in bytes        | in bytes        | key size in bytes  |
+=======================+=================+=================+=================+====================+
| TPM_MLKEM_512         | `0x0001`        | 800             | 768             | 32                 |
+-----------------------+-----------------+-----------------+-----------------+--------------------+
| TPM_MLKEM_768         | `0x0002`        | 1184            | 1088            | 32                 |
+-----------------------+-----------------+-----------------+-----------------+--------------------+
| TPM_MLKEM_1024        | `0x0003`        | 1568            | 1568            | 32                 |
+-----------------------+-----------------+-----------------+-----------------+--------------------+
| #TPM_RC_PARMS         | error when parameter set is not supported                                |
+-----------------------+-----------------+-----------------+-----------------+--------------------+

#### TPM2B_PUBLIC_KEY_MLKEM {#sec:TPM2B-PUBLIC-KEY-MLKEM}

@tbl:tpm2b-public-key-mlkem defines the TPM2B_PUBLIC_KEY_MLKEM structure.

A TPM2B_PUBLIC_KEY_MLKEM contains an encoded ML-KEM public key, according to
Algorithm 19 (`ML-KEM.KeyGen`) of FIPS 203 [@{fips203}].

Table: Definition of (MLKEM) TPM2B_PUBLIC_KEY_MLKEM Structure {#tbl:tpm2b-public-key-mlkem}

+-------------------------------------------+-------------------------+------------------------------------------------+
| Parameter                                 | Type                    | Description                                    |
+===========================================+=========================+================================================+
| size                                      | UINT16                  | size of the buffer                             |
+-------------------------------------------+-------------------------+------------------------------------------------+
| buffer \[size\] {:MAX_MLKEM_PUB_SIZE}     | BYTE                    | the buffer                                     |
+-------------------------------------------+-------------------------+------------------------------------------------+

`MAX_MLKEM_PUB_SIZE` is the maximum public key size for any ML-KEM parameter set
supported by the TPM.

@tbl:tpmi-mlkem-parms lists the public key sizes for each parameter set in FIPS
203 [@{fips203}].

#### TPM2B_PRIVATE_KEY_MLKEM {#sec:TPM2B-PRIVATE-KEY-MLKEM}

@tbl:tpm2b-private-key-mlkem defines the TPM2B_PRIVATE_KEY_MLKEM structure.

::: Note :::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::
@tbl:tpm2b-private-key-mlkem defines the canonical TPM wire format for ML-KEM
private keys. As an implementation detail, a TPM can cache the expanded private
key in `TPM2B_PRIVATE_VENDOR_SPECIFIC` rather than re-generating it every time
it is needed.
::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::

Table: Definition of (MLKEM) TPM2B_PRIVATE_KEY_MLKEM Structure {#tbl:tpm2b-private-key-mlkem}

+-----------------+-----------------+------------------------------------------+
| Parameter       | Type            | Description                              |
+=================+=================+==========================================+
| size            | UINT16          | shall be 64                              |
+-----------------+-----------------+------------------------------------------+
| buffer\[64\]    | BYTE            | the 64-byte private seed ($d || z$)      |
+-----------------+-----------------+------------------------------------------+

### TPMU_SIGNATURE {#sec:TPMU-SIGNATURE}

A @tbl:tpmu-signature `TPMU_SIGNATURE` is a union of the various
signatures that are supported by a particular TPM implementation. The
union allows substitution of any signature algorithm wherever a
signature is required in a structure.

::: Note :::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::
All TPMs are required to support a hash algorithm and the HMAC
algorithm.
::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::

When a symmetric algorithm is used for signing, the signing algorithm is
assumed to be an HMAC based on the indicated hash algorithm. The HMAC
key will either be referenced as part of the usage or will be implied by
context.

Table: Definition of TPMU_SIGNATURE Union  {#tbl:tpmu-signature}

+----------------+------------------------------------------------+--------------------------------+
| Parameter      | Type                                           | Selector                       |
+================+================================================+================================+
| rsassa         | [TPMS_SIGNATURE_RSASSA](                       | TPM_ALG_RSASSA                 |
|                | #tbl:tpms-signatures-rsa)                      |                                |
+----------------+------------------------------------------------+--------------------------------+
| rsapss         | [TPMS_SIGNATURE_RSAPSS](                       | TPM_ALG_RSAPSS                 |
|                | #tbl:tpms-signatures-rsa)                      |                                |
+----------------+------------------------------------------------+--------------------------------+
| ecdsa          | [TPMS_SIGNATURE_ECDSA](                        | TPM_ALG_ECDSA                  |
|                | #tbl:tpms-signatures-ecc)                      |                                |
+----------------+------------------------------------------------+--------------------------------+
| ecdaa          | [TPMS_SIGNATURE_ECDAA](                        | TPM_ALG_ECDAA                  |
|                | #tbl:tpms-signatures-ecc)                      |                                |
+----------------+------------------------------------------------+--------------------------------+
| sm2            | [TPMS_SIGNATURE_SM2](                          | TPM_ALG_SM2                    |
|                | #tbl:tpms-signatures-ecc)                      |                                |
+----------------+------------------------------------------------+--------------------------------+
| ecschnorr      | [TPMS_SIGNATURE_ECSCHNORR](                    | TPM_ALG_ECSCHNORR              |
|                | #tbl:tpms-signatures-ecc)                      |                                |
+----------------+------------------------------------------------+--------------------------------+
| eddsa          | [TPM2B_SIGNATURE_EDDSA](                       | TPM_ALG_EDDSA                  |
|                | #sec:TPM2B-SIGNATURE-EDDSA)                    |                                |
+----------------+------------------------------------------------+--------------------------------+
| hash_eddsa     | [TPM2B_SIGNATURE_EDDSA](                       | TPM_ALG_HASH_EDDSA             |
|                | #sec:TPM2B-SIGNATURE-EDDSA)                    |                                |
+----------------+------------------------------------------------+--------------------------------+
| mldsa          | [TPM2B_SIGNATURE_MLDSA](                       | TPM_ALG_MLDSA                  |
|                | #tbl:tpm2b-signature-mldsa)                    |                                |

::: Note :::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::
@tbl:tpmu-signature's *mldsa*, *eddsa*, and *hash_eddsa* members are `TPM2B`
types instead of `TPMS` types, because unlike the other signature types, there
is no choice of hash algorithm to include in the signature's metadata.
::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::

### TPMT_SIGNATURE {#sec:TPMT-SIGNATURE}

@tbl:placeholder-table-200 shows the basic algorithm-agile structure when a symmetric or
asymmetric signature is indicated. The *sigAlg* parameter indicates the
algorithm used for the signature. This structure is output from commands
such as the attestation commands, `TPM2_SignSequenceComplete()`, and `TPM2_SignDigest()`. It is an input to
commands such as `TPM2_VerifySequenceComplete()`, `TPM2_VerifyDigestSignature()`,
`TPM2_PolicySigned()`, and `TPM2_FieldUpgradeStart()`.

Table: Definition of TPMT_SIGNATURE Structure {#tbl:placeholder-table-200}

+-------------------------+-----------------------------------+--------------------------------------------------------+
| Parameter               | Type                              | Description                                            |
+=========================+===================================+========================================================+
| sigAlg                  | +[TPMI_ALG_SIG_SCHEME](           | selector of the algorithm used to construct the        |
|                         | #sec:TPMI-ALG-SIG-SCHEME)         | signature                                              |
+-------------------------+-----------------------------------+--------------------------------------------------------+
| \[sigAlg\]signature     | [TPMU_SIGNATURE](                 | This shall be the actual signature information.        |
|                         | #sec:TPMU-SIGNATURE)              |                                                        |
+-------------------------+-----------------------------------+--------------------------------------------------------+


### TPMU_SIGNATURE_CTX {#sec:TPMU-SIGNATURE-CTX}

A @tbl:tpmu-signature-ctx `TPMU_SIGNATURE_CTX` allows a signature scheme to
include additional contextual information.

While the selector for this union is the signing scheme associated with the
key, note that every member of the union is a byte array, meaning that
marshaling or unmarshaling code does not need to consider the selector when
marshaling or unmarshaling a `TPM2B_SIGNATURE_CTX`.

::: Note :::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::
@tbl:tpmu-signature-ctx is illustrative. This structure is defined for the
limited purpose of determining the size of a `TPM2B_SIGNATURE_CTX`.
::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::

Table: Definition of TPMU_SIGNATURE_CTX Union  {#tbl:tpmu-signature-ctx}

+----------------------------------------+---------------+---------------------------+---------------------------------+
| Parameter                              | Type          | Selector(s)               | Description                     |
+========================================+===============+===========================+=================================+
| commitCount\[sizeof(UINT16)\]          | BYTE          | `TPM_ALG_ECDAA`           | the counter value that is       |
|                                        |               |                           | used between `TPM2_Commit()`    |
|                                        |               |                           | and the sign operation          |
+----------------------------------------+---------------+---------------------------+---------------------------------+
| id\[MAX_SM2_ID_BYTES\]                 | BYTE          | `TPM_ALG_SM2`             | Value for *ID$_A$* "identifying |
|                                        |               |                           | an entity's identity"           |
+----------------------------------------+---------------+---------------------------+---------------------------------+
| buffer\[255\]                          | BYTE          | `TPM_ALG_MLDSA` or        | optional `context` value used   |
|                                        |               | `TPM_ALG_HASH_MLDSA`      | by the algorithm                |
+----------------------------------------+---------------+---------------------------+---------------------------------+
| empty\[0\]                             | BYTE          | all other values          | all other signature schemes     |
|                                        |               |                           | do not support additional       |
|                                        |               |                           | context                         |
|                                        |               |                           |                                 |
|                                        |               |                           | ::: NOTE :::::::::::::::::::    |
|                                        |               |                           | This is defined as a zero-      |
|                                        |               |                           | length buffer so that all       |
|                                        |               |                           | members of                      |
|                                        |               |                           | `TPMU_SIGNATURE_CTX` have       |
|                                        |               |                           | the same basic type.            |
|                                        |               |                           | ::::::::::::::::::::::::::::    |
+----------------------------------------+---------------+---------------------------+---------------------------------+

### TPM2B_SIGNATURE_CTX {#sec:TPM2B-SIGNATURE-CTX}

This @tbl:tpm2b-signature-ctx sized buffer contains additional signature context
in the form of a `TPMU_SIGNATURE_CTX`.

The *context* buffer contains an appropriate value for the type of key being
used with the signature or verification operation (e.g., `TPM2_SignSequenceComplete` or
`TPM2_VerifySequenceComplete`).

Table: Definition of TPM2B_SIGNATURE_CTX Structure {#tbl:tpm2b-signature-ctx}

+-----------------------------------+---------------+--------------------------+
| Parameter                         | Type          | Description              |
+===================================+===============+==========================+
| size                              | UINT16        | size of *context*        |
+-----------------------------------+---------------+--------------------------+
| context\[size\]                   | BYTE          |                          |
| {:sizeof([TPMU_SIGNATURE_CTX](    |               |                          |
| #sec:TPMU-SIGNATURE-CTX))}        |               |                          |
+-----------------------------------+---------------+--------------------------+

### TPM2B_SIGNATURE_HINT {#sec:TPM2B-SIGNATURE-HINT}

This @tbl:tpm2b-signature-hint sized buffer contains an algorithm-specific
signature hint to be provided in `TPM2_VerifySequenceStart()`.

- For `TPM_ALG_EDDSA`, *hint* contains the encoded R value from the signature.
- For all other signature algorithms, this buffer must be zero-length.

`MAX_SIGNATURE_HINT_SIZE` is an implementation-dependent value that is the
maximum hint size across all signature verification algorithms supported by
the TPM.

Table: Definition of TPM2B_SIGNATURE_HINT Structure {#tbl:tpm2b-signature-hint}

+-----------------------------------+---------------+--------------------------+
| Parameter                         | Type          | Description              |
+===================================+===============+==========================+
| size                              | UINT16        | size of *hint*           |
+-----------------------------------+---------------+--------------------------+
| hint\[size\]                      | BYTE          |                          |
| {:MAX_SIGNATURE_HINT_SIZE}        |               |                          |
+-----------------------------------+---------------+--------------------------+

## Key/Secret Exchange

### Introduction

The structures in 11.4 are used when a key or secret is being exchanged.
The exchange may be in

-   `TPM2_StartAuthSession()` where the secret is injected for salting
    the session,

-   `TPM2_Duplicate()`, `TPM2_Import()`, or `TPM2_Rewrap()` where the secret
    is the symmetric encryption key for the outer wrapper of a
    duplication blob, or

-   `TPM2_ActivateIdentity()` or `TPM2_CreateIdentity()` where the secret
    is the symmetric encryption key for the credential blob.

Particulars are described in TPM 2.0 Part 1.

### TPMU_ENCRYPTED_SECRET {#sec:TPMU-ENCRYPTED-SECRET}

This @tbl:tpmu-encrypted-secret structure is used to hold either a Labeled KEM
ciphertext (see Part 1, "Labeled Key Encapsulation Mechanism"), or a symmetrically
encrypted value. This structure is defined for the limited purpose of
determining the size of a `TPM2B_ENCRYPTED_SECRET`.

The symmetrically encrypted value may use either CFB or XOR encryption.

::: Note :::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::
@tbl:tpmu-encrypted-secret is illustrative. It would be modified depending on the
algorithms supported in the TPM.
::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::

Table: Definition of TPMU_ENCRYPTED_SECRET Union {#tbl:tpmu-encrypted-secret}

+------------------------------------------+--------------+----------------------------+-------------------------------+
| Parameter                                | Type         | Selector                   | Description                   |
+==========================================+==============+============================+===============================+
| ecc\[sizeof([TPMS_ECC_POINT](            | BYTE         | TPM_ALG_ECC                |                               |
| #sec:TPMS-ECC-POINT))\]                  |              |                            |                               |
+------------------------------------------+--------------+----------------------------+-------------------------------+
| rsa\[MAX_RSA_KEY_BYTES\]                 | BYTE         | TPM_ALG_RSA                |                               |
+------------------------------------------+--------------+----------------------------+-------------------------------+
| mlkem\[MAX_MLKEM_CT_SIZE\]               | BYTE         | TPM_ALG_MLKEM              |                               |
+------------------------------------------+--------------+----------------------------+-------------------------------+
| symmetric\[sizeof([TPM2B_DIGEST](        | BYTE         | TPM_ALG_SYMCIPHER          |                               |
| #sec:TPM2B-DIGEST))\]                    |              |                            |                               |
+------------------------------------------+--------------+----------------------------+-------------------------------+
| keyedHash\[sizeof([TPM2B_DIGEST](        | BYTE         | TPM_ALG_KEYEDHASH          | any symmetrically encrypted   |
| #sec:TPM2B-DIGEST))\]                    |              |                            | secret value's maximum size   |
|                                          |              |                            | will be limited to the digest |
|                                          |              |                            | size of the associated        |
|                                          |              |                            | algorithm                     |
+------------------------------------------+--------------+----------------------------+-------------------------------+


### TPM2B_ENCRYPTED_SECRET {#sec:TPM2B-ENCRYPTED-SECRET}

@tbl:placeholder-table-202 defines the TPM2B_ENCRYPTED_SECRET Structure.

Table: Definition of TPM2B_ENCRYPTED_SECRET Structure {#tbl:placeholder-table-202}

# Key/Object Complex

## Introduction

An object description requires a TPM2B_PUBLIC Structure and may require
a TPMT_SENSITIVE Structure. When the structure is stored off the TPM,
the TPMT_SENSITIVE Structure is encrypted within a `TPM2B_PRIVATE`
structure.

When the object requires two components for its description, those
components are loaded as separate parameters in the `TPM2_Load()`
command. When the TPM creates an object that requires both components,
the TPM will return them as separate parameters from the `TPM2_Create()`
operation.

The TPM may produce multiple different TPM2B_PRIVATE Structures for a
single TPM2B_PUBLIC Structure. Creation of a modified `TPM2B_PRIVATE`
structure requires that the full structure be loaded with the
`TPM2_Load()` command, modification of the `TPMT_SENSITIVE` data, and
output of a new TPM2B_PRIVATE Structure.

## Public Area Structures

### Description

This clause defines the TPM2B_PUBLIC Structure and the higher-level
substructure that may be contained in a `TPM2B_PUBLIC`. The higher-level
structures that are currently defined for inclusion in a `TPM2B_PUBLIC`
are the

-   structures for asymmetric keys,

-   structures for symmetric keys, and

-   structures for sealed data.

### TPMI_ALG_PUBLIC {#sec:TPMI-ALG-PUBLIC}

@tbl:placeholder-table-203 defines the `TPMI_ALG_PUBLIC` type.

Table: Definition of (TPM_ALG_ID) TPMI_ALG_PUBLIC Type {#tbl:placeholder-table-203}

+-------------------------------------+------------------------------------------------------------+
| Values                              | Comments                                                   |
+=====================================+============================================================+
| TPM_ALG_RSA                         |                                                            |
+-------------------------------------+------------------------------------------------------------+
| TPM_ALG_KEYEDHASH                   |                                                            |
+-------------------------------------+------------------------------------------------------------+
| TPM_ALG_ECC                         |                                                            |
+-------------------------------------+------------------------------------------------------------+
| TPM_ALG_SYMCIPHER                   |                                                            |
+-------------------------------------+------------------------------------------------------------+
| TPM_ALG_MLDSA                       |                                                            |
+-------------------------------------+------------------------------------------------------------+
| TPM_ALG_HASH_MLDSA                  |                                                            |
+-------------------------------------+------------------------------------------------------------+
| TPM_ALG_MLKEM                       |                                                            |
+-------------------------------------+------------------------------------------------------------+
| #TPM_RC_TYPE                        | response code when a public type is not supported          |
+-------------------------------------+------------------------------------------------------------+


### Type-Specific Parameters

#### Description

The public area contains two fields (*parameters* and *unique*) that
vary by object type. The *parameters* field varies according to the
*type* of the object but the contents may be the same across multiple
instances of a particular *type*. The unique field format also varies
according to the type of the object and will also be unique for each
instance.

For a symmetric key (*type* == `TPM_ALG_SYMCIPHER`), HMAC key (*type* ==
`TPM_ALG_KEYEDHASH`) or data object (also, *type* ==
`TPM_ALG_KEYEDHASH`), the contents of *unique* shall be computed from
components of the sensitive area of the object as follows:

$$ \mathit{unique} ≔ \mathbf{H}_\mathit{nameAlg}(\mathit{seedValue} ∥ \mathit{sensitive}) $$ {#eq:unique-parameter}

where

: {.plain .unnumbered .unlisted}

+---+-------------------------------+--------------------------------------------------------------+
|   | $\mathbf{H}_\mathit{nameAlg}$ | is the hash algorithm used to compute the Name of the object |
+---+-------------------------------+--------------------------------------------------------------+
|   | $\mathit{seedValue}$          | is the digest-sized obfuscation value in the sensitive area  |
|   |                               | of a symmetric key or symmetric data object found in a       |
|   |                               | `TPMT_SENSITIVE`.seedValue.buffer                            |
+---+-------------------------------+--------------------------------------------------------------+
|   | $\mathit{sensitive}$          | is the secret key/data of the object in the                  |
|   |                               | `TPMT_SENSITIVE`.sensitive.any.buffer                        |
+---+-------------------------------+--------------------------------------------------------------+

#### TPMU_PUBLIC_ID {#sec:TPMU-PUBLIC-ID}

This @tbl:placeholder-table-204 is the union of all values allowed in in the
*<u>unique</u>* field of a `TPMT_PUBLIC`.

::: Note :::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::
The derive member cannot be unmarshaled in a `TPMU_PUBLIC_ID`. It
is placed in this structure so that the maximum size of a
`TPM2B_TEMPLATE` will be computed correctly.
::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::

Table: Definition of TPMU_PUBLIC_ID Union  {#tbl:placeholder-table-204}

+-----------------+------------------------------------+--------------------------------+------------------------------+
| Parameter       | Type                               | Selector                       | Description                  |
+=================+====================================+================================+==============================+
| keyedHash       | [TPM2B_DIGEST](                    | TPM_ALG_KEYEDHASH              |                              |
|                 | #sec:TPM2B-DIGEST)                 |                                |                              |
+-----------------+------------------------------------+--------------------------------+------------------------------+
| sym             | [TPM2B_DIGEST](                    | TPM_ALG_SYMCIPHER              |                              |
|                 | #sec:TPM2B-DIGEST)                 |                                |                              |
+-----------------+------------------------------------+--------------------------------+------------------------------+
| rsa             | [TPM2B_PUBLIC_KEY_RSA](            | TPM_ALG_RSA                    |                              |
|                 | #sec:TPM2B-PUBLIC-KEY-RSA)         |                                |                              |
+-----------------+------------------------------------+--------------------------------+------------------------------+
| ecc             | [TPMS_ECC_POINT](                  | TPM_ALG_ECC                    |                              |
|                 | #sec:TPMS-ECC-POINT)               |                                |                              |
+-----------------+------------------------------------+--------------------------------+------------------------------+
| mldsa           | [TPM2B_PUBLIC_KEY_MLDSA](          | TPM_ALG_MLDSA or               |                              |
|                 | #sec:TPM2B-PUBLIC-KEY-MLDSA)       | TPM_ALG_HASH_MLDSA             |                              |
+-----------------+------------------------------------+--------------------------------+------------------------------+
| mlkem           | [TPM2B_PUBLIC_KEY_MLKEM](          | TPM_ALG_MLKEM                  |                              |
|                 | #sec:TPM2B-PUBLIC-KEY-MLKEM)       |                                |                              |
